# RC alignment log - the record of each Vue 3.6 RC cycle

The working record of the rc.8 (2026-09-14) and rc.9 (2026-09-21) alignment
cycles, and of the rc.10 cycle from its reading pass (2026-09-30, s35): what
was read, measured, decided and retracted, in the order it happened. It is long
on purpose and not the place to start.

- **What to do when a new RC lands:** [rc-alignment.md](./rc-alignment.md).
- **What each cycle changed:** `CHANGELOG.md`, and the alignment table in
  whitepaper section 9.
- **Why a decision was taken:** `docs/decisions.md`.

Section numbers below (s10.1, s19.4, s27, s33, s34.19, ...) are cited by tests
and scripts; keep them stable. Moved here from the repository root on
2026-09-24 (it was `rc-alignment-work.md`). Dev-only: not in `package.json`
"files", never published.

## 0. The goal, as stated by the owner (do not lose this)

- vapor-chamber must be usable in **Vue and non-Vue environments**: Vue Vapor,
  Vue VDOM, mixed trees, and no Vue at all (Blade/sprinkled JS, IIFE/CDN, Node).
- An RC cycle is about **what changed in VUE** and how it lets this project be
  **aligned, improved and better** - "unaffected / pass-through" is NOT the
  goal, it is at most a starting fact.
- Owner's open design question: does serving Vapor + non-Vapor + non-Vue from
  one package hurt us? Should non-Vue support live only at the plugin level, or
  another pattern, given rc.8? This is an ANALYSIS-BEFORE-CODE task.
- Owner discipline (repeated this session): read files and docs IN FULL, never
  grep-skim to conclude; understand code before measuring; no conclusions from
  numbers or greps alone; mark every claim VERIFIED or TO VERIFY.

## 3. rc.8 itself - fully read

Source: `https://github.com/vuejs/core/compare/v3.6.0-rc.7...v3.6.0-rc.8.patch`
(9,766 lines). Session copy was at the (ephemeral) scratchpad
`.../scratchpad/rc8/compare.patch` - refetch if gone.

25 commits, not the 14 in the release notes (11 unlisted: refactors 08, 09,
14, 16-20; ci 21; build 22; release 25). `packages/reactivity` src untouched.

- 01 compiler: createElement-backed children in templates emitted via genSelf.
- 02 events merge across prop sources (static @x + v-on object both fire).
- 03 v-once rework: new public `withOnce` (runtime-vapor), `inOnce` ambient;
  v-once snapshots props AND slot set; slot content stays live; directive
  helpers (v-show/v-model/custom) wrapped in withOnce; vdom mount is a boundary.
- 04 rawProps copied (not mutated) when injecting fallthrough attrs.
- 05 dynamic component branch keyed by RESOLVED component.
- 06 `is="vue:X"` on native tags resolves to component.
- 07 element fallback created in SVG/MathML namespace (new flags NS_SVG/NS_MATHML).
- 08 `:key` on <component :is> passed as 5th arg `key` to createDynamicComponent
  (no keyed fragment); DynamicFragment.branchKey; KeepAlive caches by it.
- 09 vnode branch mounts via interop `mountDynamicVNode`; invalid :is types
  (non string/object/function/symbol) -> dev warn + empty branch; skip branch
  closure on unchanged key.
- 10 declared class/style/event PROPS merge across sources (vdom mergeProps parity).
- 11 async setup registers with nearest (inner) Suspense.
- 12 never-mounted component instances are now disposed (scope.stop was inside
  the mounted-only branch before -> setup scope/onScopeDispose never ran).
- 13 perf: no hydration-boundary closure on client render.
- 14 one effective-root descent (FallthroughResolveState is the visitor).
- 15/16 SlotFragment honors move semantics; fragment `move()` split from insert.
- 17 DynamicFragment.current written before leave/defer; prevKey to KeepAlive.
- 18 `RenderContext` object replaces slotOwner/slotBoundary/slotScopeIds/
  parentSuspense globals; `withRenderContext` equality fast path.
- 19 slot hydration moved to dom/hydrateFragment.ts.
- 20 async `<script setup vapor>` returns template as a render closure run after
  setup settles; runtime-vapor `withAsyncContext` removed; `vue` with-vapor
  entries now export runtime-core withAsyncContext.
- 21 CI: Vue now fails its build if Vapor CSR bundles contain hydration markers.
- 22 build: enum members imported under alias inlined.
- 23 perf: patchDynamicProps, mergeProps off fallthrough path.
- 24 perf: dev-only fallthrough bookkeeping gated by __DEV__.

## 6. Architecture as understood (from the full reads)

Audiences / entries:
- Root `vapor-chamber` (src/index.ts): bus core + plugins + transports + http +
  schema + form + composables + Vapor wrappers. Reaches Vue ONLY via the
  runtime probe in chamber.ts (sync global slots `__VAPOR_CHAMBER_VUE__` then
  `__VUE__`, async bare `import('vue')`, then `@vue/reactivity` for untracked).
  In a production bundle the probe cannot resolve.
- `vapor-chamber/vue` (src/vue.ts): static imports of the Vue 3.5-safe list +
  pauseTracking/resetTracking; configureVue() on import (sideEffects-listed).
- `vapor-chamber/vapor` (src/vapor.ts): statically wires createVaporApp,
  defineVaporComponent, defineVaporAsyncComponent; re-exports ./vue. CE and
  interop plugin opt-in via configureVue (merges).
- router (`./router`, `/vdom`, `/vapor`, `/remote`), `./store`: import `vue`
  statically, never the registry.
- signal.ts: standalone, no side effects; own sync probe reads ONLY
  `globalThis.__VUE__` (not the library-owned slot); falls back to plain
  `{ value }`; `configureSignal` is called by chamber applyVueModule (probe or
  configureVue) or by `configureAlienSignals` (non-Vue reactive option).
- IIFE: `__VC_IIFE__` folds probe away; configureVue exposed on full/elements.
- vite-hmr: serve-only; primes `__VAPOR_CHAMBER_VUE__` with the full Vue
  namespace in dev (widens dev/prod gap; not a production mechanism).

## 7. Findings and hypotheses (with status)

VERIFIED
- router/vapor.ts calls `createDynamicComponent(getter)` with no props/flags/
  key; getter returns a __vapor component, a createSlot block, or null. rc.8
  changes (Block return, 5th arg, resolved-key, invalid-type guard, rawProps
  copy) do not break it. Its own `__vapor` mode guard is still required
  (objects still route to component creation without interop).
- rc.8 commit 12: before rc.8, a component created but never mounted (sibling
  render threw) never had its scope stopped -> our onScopeDispose-based
  cleanups (useCommand register/on, store holders, etc.) leaked past unmount.
  Fixed upstream; we inherit it. (Effect on us inferred from the diff; a
  real-mount fixture that fails on rc.7 would pin it.)
- The example vapor-sfc imports composables from the ROOT; it is reactive in
  prod only because main.ts imports `vapor-chamber/vapor` (which runs vue.ts
  -> configureSignal(shallowRef) on the shared chamber module).

TO VERIFY (do not state as fact)
- H1 (highest priority, bears on the Vue/non-Vue goal): a consumer importing
  composables ONLY from the root, in a production bundle, gets plain
  non-reactive `{ value }` signals (probe never calls configureSignal; __VUE__
  is a boolean after mount). untracked() and Vapor detection are pinned under
  prod conditions; signal reactivity is NOT pinned by any test read so far
  (tests not yet fully read - check signal-race-warning / reactive-fallback /
  chamber.test first).
- H2: rc.8 02/10 event merging changes `<Transition v-bind="t" @enter=...>`:
  both handlers likely fire now in Vapor (vdom parity). Needs a real-mount
  fixture; check whether VaporTransition declares on* as props.
- H3: composables after `await` in async `<script setup vapor>` under Suspense
  (rc.8 commit 20): no test seen covering it; useVaporAsyncCommand docs show
  that pattern.
- H4: outlet guard -0.07: which arm moved rc.7->rc.8 is UNKNOWN.
- H5: vitest.vapor.config alias justification ("vitest bare vue has no Vapor")
  not re-verified on rc.8.
- H6: signal.ts sync probe ignores the library-owned slot (dev/HMR-only
  inconsistency).

IMPROVEMENT CANDIDATES from rc.8 techniques (proposals only, need benches):
- defineVaporCommand allocates `untracked(() => bus.dispatch(...))` per call on
  a path documented zero-overhead; same allocation class rc.8 removed in
  3227d3b / 09. Also untracked() could get an equality/no-active-effect fast
  path like withRenderContext/withOnce. Requires bench-first per CONTRIBUTING.
- Marker-based tree-shake check (Vue commit 21 technique): we have
  esm-treeshake (Blade consumer) and router vapor-boundary (binding-level);
  a Vapor-consumer marker check may or may not add value - read
  tests/router/vapor-boundary.test.ts in full first.

Nothing in src is removable because of rc.8 (probe, registry, static lists,
mode guard, hasInjectionContext gate, directive warning, exports tripwires).
Per owner: that is not the bar - the bar is aligned/improved.

## 8. Design question to analyse next (owner's)

Options sketched (no decision; v2 "identity" decision in ROADMAP touches it):
A) keep current shape, document; B) root becomes Vue-free (bus, plugins,
transports), Vue only via `/vue` `/vapor` static entries, probe kept for IIFE/
no-build; C) B plus an `app.use(...)` Vue plugin on `/vue` providing a per-app
bus (would also address the documented concurrent-SSR shared-bus limit).
Evidence to gather first: H1, how non-Vue consumers use signal()/transports/
form today (configureAlienSignals path), ROADMAP "Posture" and v2 checklist.

## 10. Downstream feedback (a private consumer), checked against the source

**TAKE WITH A GRAIN OF SALT - EXPLORE, DO NOT ACT ON IT DIRECTLY.** This is
one consumer's report plus my reading of it. Every item is a lead to explore,
not a decision: re-read the modules yourself, reproduce through the public API,
and weigh it against the library's goal and posture before proposing anything.

Rule: the consumer is never named in repo docs/CHANGELOG (memory
no-removal-on-zero-consumers / composition plan). Items below are checked by
READING the modules listed (all read in full this session). "TO VERIFY" =
needs a test through the public API before any claim or change.

### 10.1 Reported bugs

B1 retry() retries a 422 - CONFIRMED BY READING (plugins-io.ts
`defaultIsRetryable`, transports.ts createHttpBridge catch).
- Path: postCommand does NOT retry a 4xx (http.ts guards with classifyError),
  it throws an HttpError. The bridge catches it and returns
  `{ ok:false, error }` where `error.code` is the BACKEND's body code (a string
  not starting `VC_`) or absent. `defaultIsRetryable` retries any error whose
  code is not a string or not `VC_...`. So a 422 re-enters the bridge and the
  write is re-sent `maxAttempts` times. The HTTP layer and the retry plugin
  disagree about the same failure.
- Proposed fix "use classifyError as the default": RIGHT DIRECTION, WRONG AS
  STATED. classifyError calls anything without a response status transient,
  so a plain replacement would start retrying permanent BusErrors
  (VC_VALIDATION_FAILED, VC_CORE_SEALED, ...) that today stop immediately, and
  it calls 429/408 permanent while http.ts RETRY_STATUS retries them.
- Better alternative: keep the VC_ branch (RETRYABLE_CODES), and for errors
  carrying an HTTP status use the SAME status set http.ts already retries on
  (408, 429, 5xx) - one shared predicate in http-errors.ts, used by http.ts,
  cache.serveStaleOnError and retry(). Status is available as
  `error.status` / `error.response.status` on bridge errors (both set).
- Related, found while checking (TO VERIFY): an aborted request reaches
  retry() as a DOMException AbortError whose `code` is the NUMBER 20, so
  `typeof code !== 'string'` makes it retryable; retry then re-invokes next()
  up to maxAttempts with backoff sleeps (postCommand re-throws immediately on
  the aborted signal, so nothing is re-sent, but the caller waits out the
  backoff). The downstream rule "retry AbortError" is the opposite of what a
  user abort needs; they probably mean timeouts (TimeoutError, which the HTTP
  layer already retries). Check with them.
- Also missing: retry() has no method notion; the HTTP client defaults
  mutations to retry 0 for exactly this reason. Worth documenting next to
  noRetry / idempotent.

B2 "No handler registered ... Call bus.register(...) first" - CONFIRMED
(command-bus.ts handleMissing). When a transport plugin is installed but its
`actions` filter did not match, next() falls through to the handler lookup and
the message points at the wrong fix.
- Constraint: this string is in the core, not DEV-gated, and the Blade
  tree-shake ceiling is 6,590 B with the bundle at 6,588 (esm-treeshake.test).
- Proposal: append a DEV-only hint when the bus has plugins ("or a transport
  plugin's `actions` filter did not match"). Folds out of production; costs
  ~0 in the gated bundle. Alternative (bigger): a distinct code for "reached
  the end of the plugin chain unhandled", rejected unless measured.

### 10.2 Gaps

G1 Policy per plugin, not per action - TRUE. Every plugin takes its own
`actions` glob. A per-action policy table (retry/idempotent/timeout/noRetry)
is new capability (feature freeze: needs the v1.17 template - cost, what it
buys, fixtures). Cheaper alternative first: a documented composition recipe,
or a tiny `policy({ pattern: plugin })` composer that routes by glob.

G2 No read/query layer - PARTLY WRONG. Exists: `bus.query` + `useCommandQuery`
(keeps the previous `data` on error: runDispatch only writes on success, so
"keep last good" already holds there), the http client's `cache.staleTtl`
(stale-while-revalidate) + `serveStaleOnError`, router loaders +
`router.isRevalidating`, and `cache()` plugin for bus reads. Missing: a
composable exposing freshness state (stale/revalidating) for NON-router reads.
Consider generalizing, not adding a second read system.

G3 Refresh reads after a write - TRUE outside the router. `revalidateRoutes`
does exactly this for route loaders. Alternative: the same map shape for the
bus `cache()` plugin (`invalidates: { 'cart*': ['cartGet'] }`) - reuses an
existing pattern instead of a new layer.

G4 result.error has several types - TRUE. Seen by reading: BusError,
bridge-built Error with status/code/response, raw HttpError/TimeoutError,
DOMException AbortError, and whatever a handler throws (tryCatchHandler casts
`e as Error`, so a thrown string/object is passed through). Proposal:
normalize at the core = breaking-ish. Alternative: export a small
`describeError(err) -> { message, code?, status?, detail? }` helper and
document the shapes.

G5 CSRF reuse / upload progress - HALF WRONG. `readCsrfToken` and
`invalidateCsrfCache` ARE exported from the root, and `createHttpClient().post(
url, formData, { csrf: true })` already sends FormData with CSRF and 419
refresh. The real gaps: `refreshCsrfOnce` is internal (419 recovery only via
the client), and upload PROGRESS is impossible on fetch (needs XHR). The
downstream upload.js can use the client today, minus progress.

G6 Plugin ordering by raw priority numbers - TRUE, minor. Documented numbers
already exist informally (outbox 200, idempotent 100, transport default 0).
Cheapest alternative: export named constants; phases are a larger change.

G7 Spinner delay - agree: app concern, stays in the app.

### 10.3 Wishes

W1 Idempotency ack from server - bridge could surface a response header
(e.g. a replayed flag) on the result; small, needs a convention.
W2 `inspectAction(action)` - `inspectBus` exists, but plugin `actions`
filters are closures and not introspectable; would need plugins to declare
metadata. Not cheap.
W3 DEV warning when Vue is present but untracked is not wired - ALREADY
EXISTS: `untracked()` warns once in DEV on the probe path (chamber.ts,
pinned by tests/untracked-production.test.ts). Tell them.
W4 Job handle with progress - new capability, park.
W5 Fake transport for tests - `createTestBus` records and stubs handlers but
does not model a transport; a small `fakeTransport({ action: result })`
plugin would be cheap. Evaluate against testing.ts's scope.

### 10.4 What this means for the goal (Vue AND non-Vue)

All ten items are in the framework-agnostic layer (bus, plugins, http,
transports) - the layer the non-Vue audience uses directly. B1 and G4 are the
two that can silently misbehave for every audience. Order of work proposed:
B1 (+ abort sub-finding) with real-path tests, B2 (DEV hint), W3 (reply only),
then G2/G3/G4 design notes before any code.

### 11.2 Hypotheses
- H1 MECHANISM VERIFIED (reading), consequences TO VERIFY (fixture). Root-only
  consumer in a prod bundle: probe import rejects -> applyVueModule never runs
  -> configureSignal / onScopeDispose / hasInjectionContext unset; signal.ts
  syncProbe sees __VUE__ undefined or `true` -> plain { value }. Expected:
  composable signals non-reactive, tryAutoCleanup arms nothing (on/register
  outlive unmount), KeepAlive guard inert, Vapor template binds truthy plain
  object. Unpinned by any test. Docs lead users there: README Vapor-mode
  contexts 1/2 + composables sample + "Gotcha" (waitForVueDetection cannot
  help in prod), pattern-2/3, laravel.md Inertia, vue-vapor-component.vue.
  vapor-sfc works only because main.ts imports /vapor.
- H2 VERIFIED (reading rc.8 dist + patches 02/10). rc.7: VaporTransition
  declares on* props (TransitionPropsValidators, vapor dist 4387); declared
  prop resolution returned first match with v-bind source winning -> a user's
  own @enter next to v-bind="t" was silently dropped. rc.8: merged into an
  array per raw key; core callAsyncHook waits for done if any handler has >1
  arg (core 1270), done once-guarded (1298-1307). No code change; docs note.
- H3 VERIFIED (reading). rc.8 async vapor setup uses runtime-core
  withAsyncContext (getCurrentGenericInstance, restore sets instance + its
  scope, core 3171-3183 / 6066): tryAutoCleanup and the KeepAlive guard arm
  after await. Unmount during await: stopped scope is restored, onScopeDispose
  appends without an active check (reactivity 1641), reset() runs cleanups a
  microtask later (1594-1603, 1505-1510) -> no leak. CSR shape same on rc.7;

- H6 VERIFIED as inconsistency: signal.ts syncProbe ignores the owned slot;

performance.md says signal auto-detects ref() (it wires shallowRef).

### 11.5 Design question - recommendation
Non-Vue audience is served cleanly (esm-treeshake: 0 probe refs; IIFE folds
untrack). Every shipped prod-only bug plus H1 comes from the ROOT serving Vue
users through the probe. rc.8 supports "Vue only via static entries": with-vapor
bundler entry 18 names byte-identical rc.5-rc.8; Vue consolidating on
runtime-core (withAsyncContext, RenderContext); commit 12 makes cleanup rely on
Vue scope ownership. Recommend A now (docs through /vue|/vapor, H1 fixture,
loud prod warning), B/C (composables out of root; app.use per-app bus) into the
v2 identity decision.

B1 confirmed; library docs themselves stack retry() before the bridge

### 11.8 Owner reorder: BATCH 1 = version-independent fixes (before any rc.8 work)
Definitions (proposed, from repo precedent - awaiting owner confirmation):
- WRONG (defect): code contradicts its own documented contract; or breaks an
  invariant the library states for itself (bounds.ts rule, "one named rule" in
  http-errors.ts); or the same event behaves differently by path; or docs
  describe what the code does not do.
- FIX: smallest change restoring contract = code; test that FAILS before; docs
  in the same commit; bytes/ns measured and stated; no new capability, no new
  public API, no change to documented behaviour (else it is a DECISION).
- Correctness over bytes and speed, per precedent (buildRunner 13.5%,
  headersToObject "correctness over the byte", prototype-key sweep). Speed work
  is a separate, bench-first track; none of batch 1 is speed.
- Downstream agent items: leads only; enter only on our own evidence, judged
  against OUR contract (memory downstream-agent-reports-are-leads).

Batch 1 (provenance: A = agent lead, own = own finding):
1. H1 (own): fixture under prod conditions first; fix = loud prod warning +
   correct every doc sample. DECISION: v2 B/C.
2. B1 (A, stands on own evidence): docs recommend retry()+bridge, which breaks
   http-errors' "4xx never transient". Fix = docs. DECISION: status-aware
   retry() default.
3. 429/408 two rules in http.ts (own): fix = one source of truth. DECISION:
   is 429 transient.
4. Abort via retry backoff (own): pre-flight abort not retried, mid-flight
   retried -> fix: abort non-retryable.
5. HTTP timeout NaN/Infinity fire at once (A, narrowed): violates bounds.ts;
   fix = countOption(..., MAX_TIMEOUT_MS). timeout: 0 NOT a defect (repo
   precedent floors, transitions.ts); axios framing withdrawn.
6. classifyError documented, not exported (A, own evidence): fix = README.
   DECISION (optional): export.
7. H6 owned slot ignored by signal.ts (own): fix; bytes vs ceiling.
8. Docs list s11.4 (own). Tests: H1, B1 real path, abort, timeout, stamp
   discrepancy 19.91 vs 20.21 on rc.7.
Settled with owner (2026-09-14): dropping Vue 3.5 buys little. The /vue vs
/vapor split is a COST boundary, not only a 3.5 one: a static import is
retained whether called or not (vapor.ts measured table), so wiring
createVaporApp into /vue would put the Vapor runtime in VDOM-only 3.6 apps
(size TO VERIFY). Keep 3.5 in the peer range; nothing in batch 1 needs it gone.
Document the split as a cost boundary.

Proposed (pending owner): Vue-alignment principles as decision criteria -
static `vue` imports only in Vue entries; one mode-agnostic Vue entry + opt-in
Vapor entry; interop/CE always opt-in; no instance access, scope + injection
only; app-scoped over module-global (v2). H1 = runtime detection now + Vite
plugin build-time wiring (feature template). Self-harm lessons (s11 chat):
never guess, prod-condition fixtures, real mounts, test recommended
combinations, stamp or test every claim.

Out of batch 1: B2 (enhancement, parked), V1 note (optional), all rc.8 items
(H2, H3, commit 12, H4 guard, example shrink, bumps/stamps/CHANGELOG).

### 11.9 Full record of the design discussion (self-contained; no chat needed)

#### Vue's direction (from source/patches/fixtures read, not roadmap prose)
- Two runtimes coexist; Vapor opt-in per component; VDOM not retired
  (bundler entry exports runtime-dom + runtime-vapor, byte-stable rc.5-rc.8).
- Old components bridged by an opt-in plugin, costly (vaporInteropPlugin ~78 KB
  raw, vapor.ts table); rc.8 invested in interop parity (commits 02, 09, 10).
- No component instance to userland (core 6045; maintainer statement in
  docs/router.md).
- Context via scope + injection, generic over both runtimes (core 820, 3171).
- Cleanup owned by Vue scopes (rc.6 HMR render scopes, rc.8 commit 12).
- Lesson vs the Angular 1->2 break: same Composition API on both runtimes,
  opt-in migration per component, nobody forced. For glue: one API, identical
  in VDOM/Vapor/mixed, never break a working prod app on upgrade.

#### Our decisions vs that direction
ALIGNED: KeepAlive gate on hasInjectionContext; cleanup via getCurrentScope /
onScopeDispose; router + store static `vue` imports; Vapor-native + vDOM
outlets, interop never implicit; /vapor leaves interop + CE opt-in.
DIVERGENT: root reaches Vue via runtime dynamic import() (= H1; Vue ecosystem
libs import vue statically - general knowledge, not verified here).
PARTLY: module-global shared bus (Vue scopes to the app; option C / v2).
GAP: v-vc:command has no Vapor port and warns under Vapor, while Vue supports
Vapor custom directives (withVaporDirectives, vapor-directives-fixture).

#### Self-inflicted vs Vue-originated harm (incidents recorded in files read
this session; CHANGELOG not fully re-read, so not exhaustive)
Self-inflicted, shipped: v1.6.0 vapor-sfc prod blank (probe); v1.17.0
hasInjectionContext missing (probe + hand list); H1 (probe, fixture pending);
rc.4 KeepAlive guard inert in Vapor (getCurrentInstance guess); __VUE__ recipe
clobbered by Vue (used Vue's key); Transition bridge wrote phase/dispose into
the DOM since v1.1.0 (mock-only tests); four flag-across-await bugs (sync
ping-pong, redo double record, mcp origin, reactions); shared plugin cursor
made retry() attempt 2 skip the bridge (speed-motivated shortcut);
prototype-key class at four sites; B1 (our docs recommend an unsafe
combination of our own parts).
Self-inflicted, docs/process: "directives VDOM-only" in 4 places + runtime
warn (false); router.md delete-the-guard instruction (wrong); numbers drifted
(randomUUID ~10x, 7 of 9 size rows); "on the roadmap" 12 minors after
shipping; a test that could not fail (happy-dom import.meta.url); outlet guard
fires on improvements (measures a difference).
Vue-originated: rc.6 HMR listener leak (dev); never-mounted disposal (<= rc.7);
Vapor listener dropped next to v-bind (<= rc.7, H2); upstream prod-only
slot-fallback bug (2473b78); rc.8 interop shrink trips our guard. All fixed
upstream, none needed our code; the s9 log is mostly "pass-through".
Rough ratio of recorded incidents: ~15 self-inflicted to ~4 Vue-originated;
every prod-only failure was ours.

#### Five rules from that record (proposed batch-1 criteria)
1. Never guess the environment; wire statically (entries, Vite plugin); where
   a guess is unavoidable, make its failure loud.
2. Anything crossing a build boundary gets a fixture under PROD conditions.
3. Fixtures mount the real thing and fail before the fix.
4. For glue, the combinations are the product: every combination the docs
   recommend gets a test.
5. Stamp or test every claim; unchecked prose drifts.
Performance: real wins removed repeated work (shallowRef ~3.4x array state;
transition hook names ~15x segment; useCommandGroup name cache ~-73%); the one
speed shortcut (shared cursor) was a correctness bug. Correctness first, then
remove repeated work, bench committed before/after, claims stamped.

#### H1 "make it detectable" - agreed shape (pending owner go)
(a) NOW, a fix: at composable call, `__VUE__ === true` (Vue sets it on app
creation in prod too - vapor dist 4177) + empty registry => log once, not
DEV-gated (vueDetectionHint precedent). DEV: widen the existing untracked()
probe-path warning to fire on first composable call and name reactivity,
cleanup and the KeepAlive guard. Bytes on the root barrel only (Blade bundle
tree-shakes chamber.ts).
(b) With Vite (the owner's target): the vapor-chamber/vite plugin, today
serve-only, wires /vue (or /vapor when any SFC compiles vapor) at BUILD time
so root imports are prod-correct with no import change. Mechanism TO VERIFY
(resolveId redirect vs injected entry import). New behaviour -> feature
template. (a) still needed for non-Vite bundlers and misconfiguration.

#### B1 batteries-included pattern (proposed)
HTTP retry lives on the bridge (retry + noRetry; same Idempotency-Key per
attempt; Retry-After honoured). retry() is for non-HTTP async work, and its
default becomes status-aware so stacking stays safe: VC_ codes via
RETRYABLE_CODES (unchanged); errors carrying an HTTP status retried only if
retryable by the 408/429/5xx rule; everything else retried (unchanged).
Narrows documented behaviour only for status-carrying errors -> DECISION.

#### 429 rule fix (proposed)
Inside http.ts retry behaviour is consistent (429/408 retried by the explicit
status path; classifyError only consulted after the final throw). Real issues:
serveStaleOnError will not serve on 429/408; README contradicts itself
("covers 5xx/429/408" vs "never transient... 429"); http-errors.ts claims to
be the single rule. Fix: http-errors.ts owns TWO named predicates with reasons
- isRetryableStatus (408/429/5xx, today's RETRY_STATUS) and classifyError
(stale-serve: timeout/no response/5xx); README states both; retry() (B1) uses
the first. No behaviour change unless stale-on-429 is wanted (recommend no).

#### Timeout NaN/Infinity (owner: not sure) - facts
NaN -> setTimeout fires at 0 -> immediate abort -> GET retried twice ->
TimeoutError. Infinity -> > 2^31 ms clamped ~1 ms -> immediate. NaN arises
from Number(config.X) on a missing value - the class the earlier numeric sweep
fixed elsewhere; http.ts was missed. Trade: postCommand is in the Blade bundle
(6,588 / 6,590 B); importing countOption cost 50 B in command-bus.ts, which
therefore used `| 0`. Proposal: measure an inline clamp; fits -> fix; breaches
-> owner decides correctness vs ceiling.

#### Docs list (owner: discuss) - proposed corrections
- Root-import samples (README Vapor contexts 1/2 + composables sample,
  pattern-2/3, laravel.md Inertia, vue-vapor-component.vue,
  feature-transitions) -> composables from /vue or /vapor, bus from root.
  Ships with H1(a).
- README Gotcha -> "import from /vue or /vapor"; waitForVueDetection only for
  no-build pages. Ships with H1(a).
- README ~926 getCommandBus from /vue -> from the root.
- README HTTP section -> rewrite with the 429 fix + classifyError (#6).
- Whitepaper 11.6 alias claim -> removed in v1.17, and why.
- tests/vapor/router-composables header -> same correction as
  vitest.vapor.config.ts.
- Whitepaper 11.9 "sentinel-keyed" -> "keyless: reuse follows
  resolved-component identity".
- chamber-vapor.ts JSDoc h() in Vapor setup -> return DOM nodes.
- performance.md "auto-detects ref()" -> wires shallowRef().

#### Decisions still OPEN (owner)
1. Adopt the alignment principles (11.8) + five rules (above) as criteria.
2. H1 = (a) now + (b) Vite plugin via feature template.
3. retry() default status-aware, or docs-only.
4. Timeout: measure inline clamp, then decide.
5. Docs rows to discuss / wording style.
6. v-vc:command Vapor port: record as gap for the rc.8 batch or later.
7. H4 guard (rc.8 batch): recommend split into own-arm ceiling + coarse
   saving floor.
Owner-approved so far: batch-1 reorder; "4 agree, 6 fix, 7 fix"; keep Vue
3.5; agent reports = leads. NOT approved: everything else in this section
(owner's "sure" on 2026-09-14 was a question, not an approval).

### 11.10 Downstream agent - latest report (leads only; consumer NOT named)
History: the downstream agent made and retracted claims at least three times
this session (timeout/retry/no-handler "bugs" -> "my misuse / opinion";
"getCurrentInstance null is unproven" -> wrong, it is VERIFIED core 6045 +
tests/keepalive-input-scope-fixture.test.ts). Rule: leads only, judged against
OUR contract (memory downstream-agent-reports-are-leads).
Its latest report, summarized:
- In its own app it moved write retries from a hand-rolled retry() plugin onto
  the bridge (`retry: 1`, `noRetry` from its per-action write policy).
- It found its backend controller stored the idempotent result only AFTER the
  action ran, with no lock, so a client-timed-out slow write was retried and ran
  twice concurrently. It added a lock on the idempotency key while an action
  runs; a concurrent retry gets 409 (a 4xx, so the bridge does not retry).
- Its open item (its app, not ours): client write timeout far below its
  longest server actions.
- It says "B1 stands": matches our own finding; changes nothing here.
- Its "the bridge gives up after 60 s" is ITS config; our bridge default is
  10_000 ms (src/transports.ts:154).
LEAD FOR US (agent lead, own evidence partial): docs/integrations/laravel.md
"Idempotency & double-submit protection" claims "even a retry that slips past
the client lands once", but its controller snippet does Cache::get -> run
action -> Cache::put on success, with no lock, so a retry arriving while the
first attempt still runs is NOT rejected. VERIFIED by reading laravel.md.
examples/laravel-backend/VaporChamberController.php NOT read - TO VERIFY.
Classification: docs defect (claim the code does not keep); candidate batch-1
item next to B1. Fix candidates: add a key lock to the snippet + example
(Cache::lock / 409 while running) or narrow the claim. Owner decision pending.

### 13.1 What s12 got right (V, all rows)
Every file:line citation in s12.1-s12.4 resolves to the claimed code on the
current tree. Nothing in s12 is wrong. Items s12 left TV that are now V:
- laravel controller (s12.3 last row): examples/laravel-backend/
  VaporChamberController.php dispatchOne() does Cache::get -> run action ->
  Cache::put on success, NO lock (lines 121-132). A retry arriving while the
  first attempt runs executes the action twice. laravel.md 707-710 claims
  "even a retry that slips past the client lands once". Docs+example defect,
  confirmed. The batch endpoint has the same shape.
- `__VUE__ = true` is written UNCONDITIONALLY in prod: runtime-vapor dist
  4174-4179 (prepareApp) and runtime-core dist 4442-4446 (baseCreateRenderer);
  only setDevtoolsHook is dev/devtools-gated. H1(a)'s trigger is sound.
- H2 `done` once-guard: runtime-core dist 1298-1309 (`called` latch on
  el[enterCbKey]); "wait if any handler has >1 arg": 1266-1272. Our onEnter /
  onLeave take (el, done), so Vue waits for ours even when a user's own
  1-arg @enter is merged beside it.
- router/vapor.ts on rc.8 createDynamicComponent (vapor dist 8128-8188): a
  null getter value -> resolveDynamicComponent(null) -> NULL_DYNAMIC_COMPONENT
  -> frag.update(undefined) = true empty branch; a createSlot block returns
  as-is (isBlock); a component object is its own branch key, and an unchanged
  key returns early at 8183. Keyless reuse on param-only navigation is Vue's
  behaviour, not ours. The mode guard stays needed: 8141 falls through to
  createComponentWithFallback for any object without interop, no diagnostic.

### 13.2 Corrections to s9-s12 (V unless marked)
1. "18 Vapor exports, byte-identical rc.5-rc.8" (s4, s11.5) is the view of
   the /[Vv]apor/ name filter in tests/vue-bundler-vapor-exports.test.ts, not
   of the entry. `vue.runtime.esm-bundler.js` is `export * from
   "@vue/runtime-vapor"`, and runtime-vapor's rc.8 export list gained
   `withOnce` (commit 03) and lost `withAsyncContext` (commit 20; the entry
   now re-exports runtime-core's through `@vue/runtime-dom`). The surface DID
   change at rc.8; the reopen condition (four wrapped APIs importable, no
   vapor subpath/condition) is unaffected. Wording fix for the s9 row only.
2. B1 recipe count: s12.3 lists two doc sites stacking retry() before the
   bridge. There are three: whitepaper 11.7 (~1034-1037),
   examples/pattern-2-laravel-vite.ts:28-36, examples/feature-transports.ts:
   15-19. Whitepaper 11.7 is broken twice: it also puts retry() + bridge on a
   SYNC `createCommandBus()`, which command-bus.ts syncUse dev-warns about and
   which makes every dispatch return a Promise (`result.ok` undefined).
   pattern-2 gets the bus right. Add both to the docs list.
3. Blade ceiling headroom is ONE byte, not "near": esm-treeshake asserts
   `toBeLessThan(6_590)` and the last measured value is 6_588. Any byte added
   to postCommand (item 5), signal.ts (H6), createHttpBridge or command-bus
   breaches it. s11.8 treats this per item; it is one decision, see 13.5.
4. H6 scope is narrower than s11.2 says. signal.ts's probe matters only when
   chamber.ts is NOT in the graph; the only writer of the owned slot is the
   HMR prime module, whose shim imports 'vapor-chamber' (= chamber.ts) in
   the same graph, so under Vite dev chamber.ts always seeds signal.ts first.
   The remaining trigger is a hand-written page that sets
   `window.__VAPOR_CHAMBER_VUE__` and loads a bundle containing signal.ts but
   not chamber.ts. Real but marginal; not batch-1 material (13.5).
5. H1(a) message must branch for the IIFE builds: there the probe is folded
   away (`__VC_IIFE__`) and "import from vapor-chamber/vue" is wrong advice;
   the right tail is the existing vueDetectionHint() ("Pass it:
   configureVue(Vue)"). Add the /vue clause only when `__VC_IIFE__` is
   undefined or false, so the string folds out of the IIFEs. IIFE budget
   headroom (scripts/check-size.mjs): full ~100 B, core/elements ~150-300 B.
6. H1(b) mechanism (TV, design only): a `resolveId` redirect of the bare
   'vapor-chamber' specifier to `/vue` cannot work (the root exports the bus,
   plugins, transports, http; `/vue` does not). Two workable shapes: (a) a
   virtual "wired root" module the plugin resolves 'vapor-chamber' to, whose
   body is `import 'vapor-chamber/vue'; export * from '<real root>'` (resolve
   the real root with `this.resolve(id, importer, { skipSelf: true })`),
   which covers every importer including SFCs and Blade-served entries; or
   (b) an `enforce: 'post'` transform that prepends the side-effect import to
   compiled output matching IMPORTS_VAPOR_CHAMBER. (a) is simpler and does
   not repeat the vite-hmr.ts lesson (the `enforce: 'pre'` SFC injection that
   never worked). Either way it must NOT carry `apply: 'serve'`, so it is a
   second plugin or a split export, and `/vapor` is chosen when plugin-vue
   reports a vapor SFC (needs a hook; TV). Feature template applies.
7. check-ascii.mjs EXTENSIONS has no `.php`, so examples/laravel-backend/*.php
   are unscanned: 22 em-dash lines across AddToCart (4), CancelOrder (1),
   config-vapor-chamber (2), ProcessCheckout (1), routes-web (7),
   VaporChamberController (7). The memory "sweep complete" is wrong for PHP.
   Fix: add '.php' to EXTENSIONS and sweep. Own finding, batch 1 (docs/tools).
8. s11.6 "stamp 19.91 vs 20.21 on rc.7": cannot be attributed. The folder
   arrived with no lockfile (memory local-git-repo), so the toolchain that
   produced 19.91 is not recoverable. Not a defect; record it in the CHANGELOG
   row as "re-measured on a regenerated lock" and stamp forward.

### 13.4 Where a settled position should yield (owner: not dogmatic)
- P1 The async `import('vue')` probe is the root cause of every prod-only
  incident in s11.9 (v1.6.0 blank page, v1.17.0 inert guard, H1) and of the
  dev/prod, vitest/prod asymmetry that hides them. ROADMAP says "the runtime
  probe stays, permanently... the only channel no-build pages have". That
  sentence is half wrong: on a no-build page the ASYNC probe can never
  resolve (bare specifier in a browser); the channel no-build pages have is
  the SYNC slot + configureVue(). The async probe serves vitest, dev servers
  and Node ESM only, i.e. exactly the environments where it makes broken
  bundles look correct. Technically strongest fix for the whole class:
  delete the async probe, keep the sync slot + configureVue() + the static
  entries. Then a root-only Vue consumer sees plain signals in DEV
  immediately, the H1(a) warning becomes the DEV signal too, the
  probe-masks-prod-only-bugs class cannot recur, and 13.3 C1 shrinks further.
  Costs: root-only consumers on Node SSR lose auto-wiring (import /vue); the
  tests that await the probe (chamber.test, others) are rewired to import
  src/vue; ROADMAP's "permanently" is reversed with the evidence above. This
  is a documented-stable surface, so v2 (identity decision), not batch 1;
  H1(a) is its 1.x precursor and stays compatible with it. DECISION.
- P2 The Blade tree-shake ceiling (one byte of headroom) should not decide
  correctness fixes item by item. The guard's own ledger says it is "a
  ratchet against sprawl, not against fixing a defect it happens to sit in
  front of" and has been raised for exactly that (6_560 -> 6_590). Proposal:
  land items 4-5 of batch 1 together, measure once, raise once by the
  measured amount with a ledger entry. DECISION (owner said "not sure" on
  timeout; this is the frame for it).
- P3 Outlet guard: the difference metric has now fired twice on
  improvements (v1.19: our DEV folding; rc.8: Vue's interop shrink, 51.9 ->
  51.1). Replace with two stamped guards that cannot fire on an upstream
  improvement: an own-arm ceiling (Vapor outlet machinery over the no-outlet
  floor <= 7.0 KB br; today 6.3) and a coarse saving floor (>= 15 KB br;
  today 19.43). Same question ("is the subpath worth existing"), answered
  without measuring the other arm's diet. Supersedes s11.9 decision 7 wording.
- P4 v-vc:command Vapor port: rc.8 wraps Vue's own directive helpers in
  withOnce (commit 03) and hardened withVaporDirectives across rc.3; Vue is
  investing in Vapor directives while our only directive warns under Vapor.
  This is the one place we diverge from a Vue capability, and the port shape
  is already pinned (tests/vapor-directives-fixture.test.ts: getter value, no
  updated hook, detached scope cleanup). Feature template; recommend it as
  the headline rc.8 alignment item rather than "record as gap".
- P5 The untracking-by-default decline (rc.4 row) rests on 3.1x; rc.7
  re-measured 1.22x / +5.4 ns. Still a 20% tax on a framework-agnostic bare
  dispatch, so the decline probably stands, but the row should say so on the
  reproducing number. Docs only.

1. s11.6 is wrong twice. (a) "reactivity dist rc.7 == rc.8 BYTE-IDENTICAL"

   vue@rc.7` left it at rc.8. Clean cmp: DIFFER (61,126 vs 60,493 B; rc.8
   build change, commit 22, src untouched). measure3's rc.7 arms were a
   mixed tree, ~1 KB br larger than a clean rc.7; its rc.8 arms are exact.
   (b) On clean trees under esbuild BOTH arms grew on rc.8 (control +683,
   routerOnly +761, vapor +893, interop +219 B br; saving 20.06 -> 19.40)
   because rc.8's runtime-vapor grew (+2,693 B raw in the Vapor arm): the
   hydration closure commit 13 removed became top-level functions esbuild
   keeps behind an `isHydrating` check (hydration-named functions in the
   Vapor arm 19 -> 28). "Our arm did not move; interop got smaller" is false.
2. The same four arms through Vite 8 / rolldown, the bundler consumers
   ship with (brotli B, rc.7 -> rc.8): control 11,427 -> 11,276; routerOnly
   20,288 -> 20,235; vapor 24,385 -> 24,551; interop 43,687 -> 45,461.
   Saving 18.85 -> 20.42 KB (+1.57); hydration identifiers in the Vapor arm
   5 -> 3. Every arm is 25-30% smaller than esbuild's. So the guard's rc.8
   failure is an esbuild artifact, rc.8 IMPROVED the outlet's case for real
   consumers, and the 19.5 bar is an esbuild number that rolldown already
   undercut on rc.7. The v1.19 stamp 19.91 is esbuild-only.
3. H1(b) PROVEN: a Vite plugin resolving bare 'vapor-chamber' to a virtual
   module `import '<dist/vue.js>'; export * from '<dist/index.js>'` yields a
   production bundle carrying both the eight-name configureVue and
   `_wireUntrack(pauseTracking, resetTracking, true)` from a consumer that
   imports the root only. Trap: Vite app builds drop entry exports, so the
   fixture must CALL a composable on a live path.
4. rc.8 export surface: runtime-vapor gained `withOnce`, lost
   `withAsyncContext` (runtime-core's is re-exported through runtime-dom);
   `export * from "@vue/runtime-vapor"` is unchanged on the bundler entry.

### 16.2 Item 2 - outlet guard on Vite, two limits - 4b40ac9
Files: tests/vapor/vapor-outlet-size.test.ts, scripts/stamp-docs.mjs,
README.md, docs/router.md, ROADMAP.md, docs/whitepaper.md.
- measure() is Vite's build(): configFile false, mode production, minify,
  es2022, write false, modulePreload false, the three __VUE_* defines PLUS
  `process.env.NODE_ENV: '"production"'` (vitest's process has
  NODE_ENV=test, which Vite would otherwise carry into the client
  replacement), input = one temp entry per arm, output es. The temp dir is
  under node_modules/.cache, not the OS temp dir: the entry's bare `vue` must
  resolve through node_modules the way a consumer's does, with no alias.
- DEVIATION, "take the one chunk": the router arms emit TWO chunks (the
  router's lazy makeBladeComponent). esbuild without splitting inlined it;
  the audit's rolldown numbers took the first (entry) chunk. The guard
  measures the ENTRY chunk, throws on any chunk that is neither the entry nor
  a dynamic entry, and prints lazy bytes (0.3 KB br in routerOnly and vapor,
  0.0 in interop, whose entry already carries defineComponent/h - so the
  saving is if anything under-counted).
- Result: control 11.0 / routerOnly 19.7 / interop 44.4 / vapor 24.0 KB br;
  saving 20.42 (identical to the audit's standalone rolldown run, which also
  confirms the NODE_ENV handling), own arm 4.23 (audit 4.21, on the batch-1
  dist). ownArm <= 5.0 and saved >= 15 both hold. VERIFIED.
- Metrics keys savedBr, savedRaw, floor, ownArm, ownArmCeiling,
  machineryVapor, machineryInterop (bar and margin dropped). stamp-docs:
  vc:outletOwnArm / vc:outletOwnArmCeiling / vc:outletFloor replace
  vc:outletBar / vc:outletMargin. Sites: ROADMAP (then-"next minor" row),

### 18.1 Item 1 - vaporChamberWire under the dev server (D1) - 47b5c2d
Files: src/vite-hmr.ts, src/chamber.ts, tests/vite-wire-plugin.test.ts,
README.md ("Which entry", two paragraphs), CHANGELOG.md (Unreleased),
docs/decisions.md (new row), docs/api and docs/BUNDLE-SIZES.md
(regenerated), package.json + package-lock.json + src/mcp.ts (1.20.1),
SECURITY.md and examples/vapor-sfc/README.md (vc:version), README and
whitepaper count stamps.
- The measurement D1 asks for (S/item1-devgraph.mjs, item1-devgraph.txt,
  item1-devgraph-optimized.txt): a real Vite 8.2.2 dev server (createServer,
  middleware mode, hmr off), a transformRequest crawl of the module graph from
  the entry, a module counted as a chamber when its SERVED code carries
  chamber.ts's DEV probe-path string (pre-bundled chunk or dist/chamber.js
  alike). Chamber modules per layout and arm:

      layout                                     control  documented  redirect
      linked (node_modules/vapor-chamber -> ..)     1         1          1
      copy installed under node_modules/.cache      1         1          1
      app root in the OS temp dir, package          1         1          2
        copied in, vue/@vue symlinked

  control = composables from the root, no plugin; documented = bus from the
  root, composables from /vue, no plugin; redirect = the plugin with its
  redirect forced to run under serve. The first two layouts never engage the
  optimizer: Vite skips optimizing any import whose IMPORTER is inside
  node_modules (vite dist chunks/node.js:32878,
  `importer && isInNodeModules(importer)`), and under node_modules/.cache
  main.js itself is. In the third, the wired root's `export *` resolved
  (this.resolve, skipSelf) to the PRE-BUNDLED /node_modules/.vite/deps/
  vapor-chamber.js, while its `import 'vapor-chamber/vue'` - importer the
  virtual module, whose id carries a node_modules path - was served unbundled
  (dist/vue.js -> dist/chamber.js): two registries, the wiring on the one the
  app does not read. The documented arm shares one chamber-*.js chunk.
  VERIFIED. -> D1's branch TWO: the redirect stays build-only; the plugin
  defines `__VC_WIRED__` under serve.
- Correction, recorded as a new dated row in docs/decisions.md (the file is
  append-only): the H1(b) row and s16.7 said a dev redirect "would pair a
  pre-bundled /vue with an unbundled root"; measured, it is a pre-bundled
  ROOT with an unbundled /vue.
- Where a dev-server define reaches (S/item1-define.mjs, item1-define2.mjs,
  .txt): Vite leaves dependency code untouched in dev - a node_modules file
  reading `typeof __VC_WIRED__` is served as written, pre-bundled or excluded;
  /@vite/env carries `const defines = {"__VC_WIRED__": true}` and assigns it
  to globalThis; vitest assigns every dot-free define to globalThis in its
  test runtime (read: vitest dist cli-api `deleteDefineConfig`, setup-common
  `setupDefines`); SSR dev with the package externalized sees neither
  (`inDep=false`, global undefined). VERIFIED.
- FINDING, verified in a real browser (the desktop app's built-in Chromium on
  a live Vite 8 dev server, the temp-dir layout, vaporChamberHMR() so Vue is
  detected in the page): `typeof process` undefined, `__VC_WIRED__` true, a
  control console.warn captured, Vue detected (`loading` a ref, Vapor on) -
  and NO [vapor-chamber] warning at all: not the Vue-free sync-bus thenable
  DEV warning, not the probe-path hint. The served dev.js reads
  `DEV = typeof process === "undefined" ? false : ...` (pre-bundled:
  `... : true`). So every DEV diagnostic in the ESM build is silent in a
  page under Vite dev, and fires where `process` exists (vitest, SSR).
  README's "In DEV it warns once ... on a page that does have Vue" was false
  for pages; corrected in "Which entry". The wider question is open (18.7).
- Code: vaporChamberWire drops `apply: 'build'`; `config(_, env)` records the
  command and returns `{ define: { __VC_WIRED__: 'true' } }` under serve;
  resolveId declines under serve; load unchanged. chamber.ts: `declare const
  __VC_WIRED__`, and warnProbePath gains `!(typeof __VC_WIRED__ !==
  'undefined' && __VC_WIRED__)`; its doc and the `_vueSubpathLoaded` note no
  longer say the hint fires "under the dev server".
- Fixture (tests/vite-wire-plugin.test.ts, extended to 12 cases): the
  measurement pinned (2 chamber modules, one under /deps/, one
  dist/chamber.js); the documented control (1, in /deps/); the shipped plugin
  under serve (1, and `"__VC_WIRED__": true` in /@vite/env); the plugin
  object under build and serve; the hint with the global unset (1) and set
  (0). The dev project copies dist without maps and .d.ts. Fails-before
  (S/item1-before.txt, src unchanged): 3 of 12 fail, each on the new
  assertion - `expected 'build' to be undefined`; `/@vite/env` without the
  define; `expected [ Array(1) ] to have a length of +0 but got 1` - and 9
  pass, the measurement and the control among them. After: 74/74 across the
  wire, root-only, mcp, vite-hmr and vite-hmr-pipeline files
  (S/item1-after.txt). VERIFIED.
- Bytes (S/bytes-1-item1.txt): IIFEs 11,581 / 7,919 / 8,416 and Blade 6,586
  unchanged (D3). dist/vite-hmr.js 6,320 -> 6,471 raw (2,124 -> 2,178 br);

### 19.4 FINDING (pre-existing, not this batch's): the ESM DEV fold is
per chunk, so a code-split app ships DEV-gated code in every chunk that
does not define DEV
Evidence, vapor-island-cart's production build on BOTH trees: the store
chunk carries `var t=!1` (DEV, folded - v1.19.0's fix works there) and
exports it; the Cart chunk imports that binding as `T` and keeps
`function U(){T&&M!==null&&...console.warn("[vapor-chamber] Vue detected
at runtime rather than at build time...` - the whole probe-path warning,
dead at runtime, shipped. Minification runs after chunking, and a minifier
cannot fold an imported binding. vapor-sfc (one chunk) carries no DEV
string; island-cart's other chunks carry none because their DEV code is in
the same chunk as the constant or was shaken for other reasons. So "a
consumer's production build folds DEV" (v1.19.0, and s18.1's byte note)
holds for single-chunk apps only. Same root as 18.7's first item (the
shape of DEV_ESM_SOURCE); design and measurement first, batch 4. Cost
today: one warning string per affected chunk (~0.7 KB raw in Cart); the
IIFEs are unaffected (their define folds at build).

      B slowAdd key=k1-1789395724 HTTP 409 t=0.010892s
      A slowAdd key=k1-1789395724 HTTP 200 t=2.025560s
      C slowAdd key=k1-1789395724 HTTP 200 t=0.007478s
      D cartAdd key=k2-1789395726 HTTP 200 t=0.004961s
      E cartAdd key=k2-1789395726 HTTP 200 t=0.004551s
      F cartAdd key=k3-1789395726 HTTP 200 t=0.005018s
      A body: {"ok":true,"state":{"count":1,"total":4,"lastAdded":"Coffee"}}
      B body: {"ok":false,"error":"A request with this Idempotency-Key is still running","code":"in_progress"}
      C body: {"ok":true,"state":{"count":1,"total":4,"lastAdded":"Coffee"}}
      D body: {"ok":true,"state":{"count":2,"total":8,"lastAdded":"Coffee"}}
      E body: {"ok":true,"state":{"count":2,"total":8,"lastAdded":"Coffee"}}
      F body: {"ok":true,"state":{"count":3,"total":12,"lastAdded":"Coffee"}}

  The concurrent duplicate got 409 in_progress while the first held the lock;
  the retry after it replayed the stored body without running the action
  (count stays 1); a sequential repeat replays (E = D); a fresh key writes
  (F). laravel.md's claim holds as written, so neither the controller nor the
  doc changed. VERIFIED for the server half; that the bridge never retries a

  import.meta.env?.DEV ? process.env.NODE_ENV !== "production" : false`.
  Precedence is `(A || B) ? C : false`; on a no-bundler page both A and B
  are falsy and C is never evaluated (no ReferenceError), which the fixture's
  no-bundler arm and check-env-guards both hold. The generateBundle guard
  errors if any chunk re-bundles src/dev.ts; the fixture pins one-chunk and
  code-split shapes byte-identical to a DEV=false build and that no ESM chunk
  imports DEV from another. Reproduced s19.4's shipped hint on the old build.

are legitimate - they were not hiding useless code, bar one line.

on the measured surface. Three files are excluded in vitest.config.ts; each
was re-examined against the owner's rule:

- **src/router/vapor.ts** - STRUCTURAL, kept. It cannot be imported under the
  default vitest config at all: its named imports (createDynamicComponent,
  createSlot, defineVaporComponent) do not exist on the `vue` build a bare
  specifier resolves to there, so the module fails to LINK. It is covered
  under vitest.vapor.config.ts (tests/vapor/vapor-outlet*), including against
  an executed production bundle. Not a guard; not touchable here.
- **src/testing.ts** (357 lines) - test-only helper (`createTestBus`), NOT a
  public subpath (absent from package.json "exports"). Covering it means
  tests that test the test helper. Legitimate exclusion, kept.
- **src/directives.ts** - measured 98.79 / 96.69 / 95.65 / 100 with the
  exclusion lifted. Each uncovered line was classified:
    - `el.ownerDocument ?? document` (line 158): DEAD. Element.ownerDocument
      is non-null in lib.dom, so the fallback is unreachable. REMOVED.
    - `resolved.error ?? null` (295): NOT dead - type-required. `resolved.error`
      is `Error | undefined`, `state.error` is `Error | null`, so `?? null`
      converts undefined to null for the assignment. Deleting it fails
      typecheck ("Type 'undefined' is not assignable to type 'Error | null'").
      Kept. (Caught by running typecheck on the deletion - the branch is
      unreachable at RUNTIME but the code is required by the TYPES.)
    - `removeDelegatedElement`'s `if (!doc)` (166) and `count === undefined`
      (168): null-guards matching the function's own `Document | null` param
      and a `Map.get` result. Real safety at a function boundary. Kept.
    - `state.delegatedDoc ?? null` (384): the null arm feeds the guard above;
      same call. Kept.
    - `handler: () => {}` (354): an init placeholder overwritten by
      `state.handler = buildHandler(el, state)` before anything calls it -
      buildHandler closes over `state`, so `state` must exist first. Structural,
      not a guard. Kept.
  So one genuinely dead branch removed; the rest is type-required or real
  defensive safety, which the owner's rule says to keep. The exclusion stays,
  its reason in vitest.config.ts tightened to say exactly this.

### 23.1 Pairing, re-read at source (the handoff's "do not re-litigate")
The handoff asked to pin, not re-open, "onBefore and the settling on('*')
are PAIRED on every dispatch exit path ... the one asymmetry only ever
DECREMENTS". Reading _syncDispatchInner, _asyncDispatchInner, buildRunner and
buildAsyncRunner found two things the premise does not cover, both now
fixture-pinned:
- More settle-without-start exits than the pre-flight abort: `query()` (no
  before-hooks by design, fans out to on('*')), `emit()` (fresh cmd to
  listeners), and a before-hook that throws AHEAD of ours. "Only decrements,
  clamped at 0" is true for stuckness but WRONG for correctness: with a
  concurrent dispatch of the same key in flight, such a settle would clear
  that dispatch's flag. Fix: the before-hook records `cmd -> slot` in a
  WeakMap and the settle acts only on a Command it started.
- One start-without-settle exit: a PLUGIN that throws or rejects. Neither
  runner catches, so dispatch throws / rejects before syncRunHooks /
  asyncRunHooks, and no listener fires. The key stays true. This is the
  path the old chamber.ts comment ("a rejected dispatch promise bypassed the
  bus's errResult fan-out") was about. NOT fixed here - it is a bus contract
  question (should a plugin throw become an errResult with hooks?), owner's
  call; pinned as "KNOWN LIMIT" in the fixture and stated in JSDoc, README
  row, performance.md, CHANGELOG and the decisions row.
The chamber.ts comment was corrected to say exactly this, citing the fixture.

### 23.2 feat-loading/1 - the primitive + the fixture (items 1 and 2) - 28b4976
Files: src/chamber.ts, tests/command-loading-fixture.test.ts (new), docs/api,
docs/BUNDLE-SIZES.md, count and size stamps (README, whitepaper,
performance.md, docs/migrating/from-mitt.md sizeIifeCore only).
- Shape per D1: SharedCommandStateEntry gains `slots` (Map<commandKey,
  LoadingSlot>), `started` (WeakMap<Command, LoadingSlot>) and `unBefore`;
  the existing on('*') observer settles; dispose unhooks the before-hook.
  `isLoading(action, target?)` returns `Readonly<Signal<boolean>>`.
- D2 atomic: one `signal(false)` per key, written only on 0 <-> 1.
- Deviation, argued: tracking is installed LAZILY at the first isLoading()
  call per bus, not at entry creation. A bus whose shared state nobody asks
  per-key questions of pays no before-hook and no commandKey per dispatch.
  Consequence, pinned: a dispatch already in flight when tracking starts is
  not counted. Also: the first isLoading() on a SEALED bus throws
  VC_CORE_SEALED (addHook asserts), documented, not caught.
- D3: pruned, cheaply - a slot handed out by isLoading() is marked `read`
  and kept (the reader holds its signal); an unread slot is deleted when its
  count returns to 0. Bound = keys ever read + keys in flight.
  Measured (S/slot-heap.mjs, node --expose-gc, dist + Vue shallowRef,
  100,000 keys; S/item1/slot-heap.txt): read key retained 272.5 B/key;
  unread keys after dispatch-and-prune 37.64 B/key residual. The residual's
  source is TO VERIFY (candidates: WeakMap ephemeron entries not yet swept,
  per-dispatch meta); it is not a slot, which the prune branch deletes.
- D5: `isLoading(action)` is the exact key `(action, undefined)`, pinned
  against a dispatch with target 'eu'. "Any target of this action" is NOT
  provided: it needs a second per-action counter, not one line. The panel's
  question ("is THIS service restarting") is the exact key.
- Fixture: real Vapor component (with-vapor browser dist, configureVue on
  the same module), each key read in its own `watchEffect({ flush: 'sync' })`,

  concurrent to 2 and back, pre-flight abort (alone and beside a live
  dispatch), handler reject, before-hook throw ahead of ours, query during
  flight, unread key pruned, lazy start, commandKey value keys, exact
  target-less key, shared signal identity, dispose unhooks, plugin-throw
  known limit.
- Fails before (S/item1/before.txt, the fixture on unchanged src; 12 cases
  at the time, the lazy-start case added after):
      Tests  12 failed (12)
      TypeError: shared.isLoading is not a function
  After (S/item1/after.txt): 75/75 across the fixture and the six suites

### 23.5 Stretch analysis - store granularity (NO code shipped)
How the store notifies today (src/store.ts, read whole): one
`shallowRef(state)` per store, replaced wholesale by every action's reducer;
consumers read `store.state.value.<field>`. Any action therefore notifies
EVERY reader of `state.value`, whichever field it reads.
Does shallowRef granularity already cover it? Partly, and for free on the
consumer side: `computed(() => store.state.value.items)` re-evaluates on
every store write but propagates only when its result changes (`===`), and a
reducer that spreads (`{ ...s, count: s.count + 1 }`) keeps untouched
fields' identities. So a component that reads through a computed re-renders
only when its field changed; the cost is one computed evaluation per reader
per store write, O(readers) per dispatch. A template reading
`state.value.items` directly re-runs on every write. So the shallowRef
design gives field-level RENDER granularity via computed, not field-level
NOTIFICATION.
What an atomic-field API would cost, by this feature's template: a signal per
top-level field (lazily, on first read, as isLoading does per key), and on
each write a per-field `!==` diff of old vs new state to decide which field
signals to write - O(fields) per dispatch instead of O(readers), no computed
per reader. Costs: the diff on every action; a second source of truth unless
`state.value` becomes a derived getter; nested fields are not covered (top
level only, like shallowRef); and the store's plugin story (persist, history)
reads whole state, unaffected. When it wins: many readers, many fields, each
reader on one field. When it does not: few readers (computed is cheaper
than a diff). Needs its own handoff with an interleaved A/B on the real
dispatch path before any claim.

- Bench markers not stamped from the main tree's local bench output (23.0).

- Plugin throw/reject: make the runners convert it to an errResult with
  hooks (fixes isLoading AND inFlight-scoped-by-wrapper), or keep the throw
  as the contract.
- The size:doc drift on main (regenerated here). The bench markers only

builds it, brotli q11); S/blade-arms.mjs (the same consumer four ways, 25.3).

### 25.1 Baseline (8b1a33b), measured
- IIFE brotli full / core / elements 11,731 / 8,058 / 8,561 (budgets 11,600 /

  is +150 / +139 / +145 and +116: all of it the two http commits (2b8e761 CSRF

### 25.2 Candidates, each measured once (no variants), verdicts
    cand  what                                   full    core    elem    Blade   verdict
    C1    BusError fields `declare`d             -268    -266    -258    -13     LANDED size/1
    C2    http status arrays -> comparisons       -11      +1     -27     -9     DECLINED (core grew)
    C3    MAX_DEPTH / REQUEST_TIMEOUT, one fn     -24      -1     -17     -1     LANDED size/2
    C4    IIFE target safari13 -> safari13.1     -202    -131    -163      0     PROPOSAL (owner)
    C5    tree-shake blockers                      -       -       -       -     none found
- C1 (d7ecc8c). The IIFE target includes safari13, which has no class fields,
  so BusError's five field declarations were lowered to defineProperty calls
  through a ~0.6 KB helper chain (`@@toPrimitive` / toPropertyKey / typeof
  helpers, visible in all three .min.js), then overwritten by the
  constructor's own assignments. `declare` keeps the types and emits only the
  assignments; `name` is assigned last so own-key order is unchanged. Less
  work per construction, so no bench needed for speed. After: the helper
  string appears 0 times in the three bundles. The biggest single item in
  this audit, and invisible to the Blade row (esbuild's esnext output keeps
  native fields), which is why it had never been seen.
- C2 (not committed). SESSION_EXPIRED_STATUS / RETRY_AFTER_STATUS `includes`
  as plain comparisons. Mixed: core IIFE +1 B. Handoff rule "land only if it
  actually shrinks" - declined, reverted, bytes back to size/1 exactly
  (S/bytes-c1-restored.txt).
- C3 (cce31a4). The two BusError results were written out in full in both
  buses; one function each, same wording. Small, all four shrink. It touches

  owner's speed rule it was benched first: a temporary three-arm A/B (base

### 25.3 The Blade ceiling: what the red is actually made of
S/blade-arms-final.txt, on cce31a4, raw / brotli:

    esbuild, no define (the test)          20,601 / 6,777
    esbuild, NODE_ENV=production define    20,601 / 6,777
    esbuild, DEV the literal false         20,346 / 6,729
    Vite 8 app build, production           18,830 / 6,126   (no DEV markers)

- FINDING 1: the handoff's proposed fix (give the test a production define)
  does NOT move this number. Under esbuild the batch 4 DEV expression becomes
  `(typeof process<"u"||import.meta.env?.DEV,!1)`: `import.meta.env?.DEV` is a
  property read esbuild keeps for side effects, so the comma expression is
  not a constant and no `DEV && ...` branch folds. The pre-batch-4 shape
  (`typeof process === "undefined" ? false : ...`) did fold under a define.
- FINDING 2 (a real shipped-bytes regression, for esbuild consumers only):
  an app bundled by plain esbuild with a production define ships every DEV
  branch and string since batch 4 - 48 B brotli in this small consumer, more
  in a larger one. Vite/rolldown folds it (the example chunks and the Vite
  row above carry none). Fixing it means an expression that is pure after
  NODE_ENV substitution AND true in a Vite dev page AND safe on a no-bundler
  page; I found no shape meeting all three (each candidate leaves a
  non-constant import.meta read). Owner's call whether esbuild-without-Vite
  consumers matter enough to revisit batch 4's D1.
- So DEV explains only 48 B of the 187 B the consumer sits over its ceiling
  after this audit. The rest is shipped correctness code (the CSRF fix and
  the runWithRetry loop, +116 since batch 4) measured by a bundler that
  compresses ~650 B worse than the one consumers use.

## 27. Performance audit (s27, 2026-09-14, branch `perf-audit`)

Handoff: perf-audit-handoff.md. Worktree off main 8b1a33b, `npm ci`, Node
24, vue 3.6.0-rc.8. Owner, mid-session: performance includes V8 alignment
(hidden classes, monomorphic ICs, allocations); the comments' measured
claims are believed, not re-measured; every code change is claimed by a
test; coverage stays 100%.

### 27.1 Harness, and what it can resolve
Three arms through the REAL bus in one process: `base` = `git show
HEAD:src/command-bus.ts` (derived from the shipped source), `self` = a
byte-identical second copy (the self-control), `cand` = the working tree.
Each is its own module instance; `gc()` before every timed segment
(`NODE_OPTIONS=--expose-gc`), arm order rotated per round, paired per-round
ratios, 15 rounds, NODE_ENV=production (DEV folded off as shipped). A driver
repeats it in separate processes (the run is the sampling unit - one process
draws one set of instances). Ten workloads: sync bare / +1 listener /
+1 afterHook / query +1 listener / 3 plugins + 1 listener / emit (control) /
mixed emit+dispatch through one '*' listener; async bare / query / 3 plugins.
Scripts: S/perf-audit-ab.test.ts, S/run-perf-ab.mjs (S = session scratchpad).

NULL RUN FIRST (src untouched, all three arms identical bytes, 3 runs,
S/pa-null.txt): two workloads still read "every run outside the self band" -
query +1 listener 1.018-1.022 "slower", async 3 plugins 0.972-0.988
"faster". So a 3-run self band is not a resolution. The harness floor is the
null run's worst deviation, ~3%; nothing inside it is claimed. A loaded
machine (load avg ~5 from other windows) widened a later run's self arm to
0.71-1.35 on identical bytes (S/pa-A.txt) - those runs are discarded, not
averaged; RUNS=7 on a quiet machine gives tight clusters.

### 27.2 ab:vue vs rc.7 - dists identical, nothing to measure
The reactivity regions of vue.runtime-with-vapor.esm-browser.js (14
`#region packages/reactivity/` blocks) differ between rc.7 and rc.8 by ONE
line, a region marker (`//#region packages/reactivity/src/system.ts` split).
@vue/reactivity's own dist differs only by the `ReactiveFlags` enum object
being inlined to literals (`ReactiveFlags$1.Dirty` -> `16`); the prod
with-vapor dist has no enum names in either version. No logic change, so
per the harness's own rule the ab:vue pass was not run.

### 27.3 Candidate A - skip the runner's `nextFrom` closure with no plugins: DECLINED
Idea: `buildRunner([])`'s `run` still allocates `nextFrom` per dispatch on
the "hooks/listeners, no plugins" path. Ratios are cand/base, < 1 faster.
- A1, a separate `(cmd, execute) => execute()` returned for the empty chain
  (RUNS=7, quiet, S/pa-A7.txt): no-plugin paths FASTER outside the floor -
  dispatch +1 listener 0.921-0.932, +1 afterHook 0.907-0.941, query +1
  listener 0.834-0.854, async query 0.901-0.940, mixed 0.949-0.963 (self
  bands at most [0.978, 1.027]) - but every plugin path SLOWER: sync 3
  plugins 1.109-1.150 (96.4 -> 108.0 ns/cmd, self <= 1.032), async 3 plugins
  1.062-1.109. Cause, V8: `s.runner(cmd, execute)` now sees two function
  shapes across buses (the empty arrow at creation, `run` after `use()`) and
  the call site goes polymorphic.
- A2, one `run` with an early `if (plugins.length === 0) return execute()`
  and `nextFrom` as an arrow after it, so the call site stays monomorphic
  (RUNS=7, S/pa-A2.txt; runs 6-7 hit a load burst, runs 1-5 read): no-plugin
  0.933-0.975, but sync 3 plugins still 1.051-1.078 and async 1.020-1.061 -
  the hoisted `function nextFrom` declaration cannot be skipped by an early
  return, and the arrow that can costs the plugin path.
Both forms trade the documented hot row ("3 plugins + 1 listener") for the
plugin-free ones. Not landed; runner restored byte-for-byte. No third variant
(finish-first rule).

### 27.4 perf/1 - one hidden class for every CommandResult: LANDED (117b839)
V8 probe (S/v8-shapes.mjs, `%HaveSameMap` on the built dist): the bus's
ok/err results share a map, but validator, authGuard, debounce and async
optimisticUndo results did NOT, nor the emit command vs the dispatch command,
nor the async command (5 fields, `signal`) vs the sync one. A grep then found
~30 hand-built result literals outside the bus (`{ ok, error }`, `{ ok,
value }`, `{ ok, error, value }`): every HTTP/WS bridge result, outbox,
runDispatch/useSharedCommandState error paths, v-vc:command, SSR, MCP,
schema, workflow steps, retry's initial result. All now call the bus's
factories, exported underscored (`_okResult`/`_errResult`, not in the
barrel, like `_stampMeta`). Speed is not claimed (rejection/transport paths,
not the dispatch loop); the claim is map identity and it SHRANK every bundle:
full 11,731 -> 11,699 br, core 8,058 -> 8,048, elements 8,561 -> 8,538,
Blade 6,791 -> 6,788. tests/v8-shapes.test.ts checks `%HaveSameMap` in
vitest (`v8.setFlagsFromString('--allow-natives-syntax')` + `new Function`)
and scans src/ for a new hand-built literal; 3 of its 4 tests fail on HEAD
src. Gates green but the pre-existing Blade ceiling (6,788 > 6,590,
owner-deferred, now 3 B less red); coverage 100 x4 (4950/3081/1040/4133).
Found on the way: HEAD's docs stamp for the Blade bundle read 6.5 KB against
a real 6,791 B; re-stamped to 6.6.

### 27.5 perf/2 - async `execute` as a plain function: LANDED
`_asyncDispatchInner` and `asyncQuery` built `async () => { ...; return
tryCatchAsyncHandler(handler, cmd); }`: an async body returning a promise
allocates a second promise, resumes a second frame and resolves via a
thenable job. Now a plain arrow returning the promise; the missing path goes
through `async function asyncMissing` so `onMissing: 'throw'` stays a
rejection to plugins (a sync throw would skip a plugin's `next().catch`).
`tryCatchAsyncHandler` stays extracted - its comment's claim is believed.
- First run (RUNS=7, all ten workloads, S/pa-B.txt) was on a busy machine:
  every self band wide; async query leaned 0.72-0.94 but overlapped its band.
  Not claimed.
- Rerun, async rows only, 21 rounds, RUNS=7 (S/pa-B2.txt, load avg ~10
  throughout - the paired design held; ratios are the claim, not ns):
    async bare dispatch   0.834-0.902  self [0.961, 1.078]  356 -> 308 ns/cmd  FASTER
    async query           0.744-0.864  self [0.968, 1.117]  358 -> 312 ns/cmd  FASTER
    async 3 plugins +1 l  0.839-0.977  self [0.946, 1.067]  overlaps - not claimed
  Both claimed rows clear the ~3% null floor in every run. The in-suite
  two-arm A/B agreed: async dispatch 1.170x, query 1.121x throughput.
BYTES - NOT size-neutral, OWNER DECISION: full IIFE 11,699 -> 11,706 br
(+7), core 8,048 -> 8,052 (+4), elements 8,538 -> 8,548 (+10); Blade 6,788 ->
6,787 (async bus shaken out). perf/1 + perf/2 together are still below main
on every IIFE (full 11,731 -> 11,706). Committed on the branch as perf/2 so
the audit can see it; revert that one commit if the owner declines the bytes.
Gates: 2164 + 24 tests (only the esm-treeshake ceiling red, pre-existing),
coverage 100 x4 (4947/3081/1041/4132), biome, ascii, stamp, doc-claims.

### 27.6 C1 - emit builds the dispatch Command shape: DECLINED
The probe showed emit's `{ action, target }` on a different map from
dispatch's `{ action, target, payload, meta }`, so a `'*'` listener reading
both is polymorphic. Candidate: pad emit's literal (both buses) with
`payload: undefined, meta: undefined`. RUNS=7, 21 rounds (S/pa-C1.txt):
    mixed emit+dispatch, '*' reads cmd  0.967-1.012  self [0.976, 1.008]  no gain
    emit 1 listener (control/cost)      1.000-1.036  self [0.983, 1.026]  leans ~3% slower
The polymorphism is real but a 2-map IC is cheap; the two extra stores on
every emit cost as much as they save. Not landed, emit restored; the
v8-shapes emit case written for it was removed with it.

### 27.7 C2 - the async command's `signal` slot: NOT BUILT, reasoned
The probe also showed the async dispatch command (`{ action, target,
payload, meta, signal }`) on a different map from the sync one. Each bus
type is monomorphic on its own, and an app normally runs one type; the only
way to unify them is a `signal: undefined` store on the SYNC hot path, which
is the class of change `_syncDispatchInner`'s comment records as 25% worse
(isBare). That comment is believed; nothing to gain that justifies the risk.
performance.md's "Command literal always `{ action, target, payload, meta }`
... across `dispatch` / `query` / `emit` / `request`" is corrected to say so.

## 28-29. Audits (s24/s26/s28) and integration to main

All three windows audited from clean detached worktrees at their heads, and
merged to main. Verdicts:
- feature-loading (per-target loading): ACCEPTED. Fails-before 13 cases;
  coverage 100 x4 (clean rebuild); the plugin-throw stuck-key finding VERIFIED
  at source as a PRE-EXISTING bus gap (the runner has no try/catch, so a plugin
  that throws in its own body escapes before the settle fires - the error
  observer misses it too), correctly documented + pinned, not a blocker.
  WeakMap start-matching and atomicity pinned. +200 B full IIFE.
- size-audit: ACCEPTED. BusError fields `declare`d (drops the Safari-13
  class-field helper, -266/IIFE, less work per construction); shared
  maxDepth/timeout result helpers (-24/-1/-17, cold paths, bench speed-neutral).
  BusError verified correct (constructor assigns all five; declare only strips
  lowering). IIFEs under budget alone.
- perf-audit: ACCEPTED. perf/1 unifies ~30 hand-built CommandResult literals to
  the two factories - one hidden class (V8 %HaveSameMap test), behaviour-
  preserving (only adds an explicit undefined field), and SMALLER. perf/2 cuts
  one async frame per command (~13% faster async dispatch/query, 7 processes,
  clears the ~3% null-floor), onMissing:'throw' rejection contract kept via
  asyncMissing and pinned. +7 B full IIFE (owner took it).

Integration (owner decisions: merge all three, take perf/2, re-measure the
ceiling with Vite + budget bump): merged in order feature -> size -> perf; the
only source conflict was chamber.ts's import line (commandKey + _errResult,
combined); the rest were regenerated stamps and the log (all sections kept).
Size reconciliation: the full IIFE brotli budget 11_600 -> 11_700 (merged
11,625; net of +200 feature and -300 size/perf; ~75 B headroom for queued
features), and tests/esm-treeshake.test.ts now measures its size ceiling on a
VITE production build - what consumers ship - instead of the esbuild no-define
number that kept ~660 B of DEV code no app receives (esbuild 6,775 vs Vite
6,116; ceiling 6_300). The esbuild builds stay for the symbol check.

Merged main is FULLY GREEN: typecheck, build, test:run 2178 passed | 1 skipped
| 0 FAILED, test:vapor 24/24, size:check under all budgets, esm-treeshake
green, lint (ascii/guards/stamps), coverage 100/100/100/100
(4976/3093/1047/4159), docs + size:doc zero drift, three examples build.
No version bump (v1.20.0 unreleased).

### 30.1 plugin-throw/1 - both runners, VC_PLUGIN_THREW, DEV log - d807255
- D1: `buildRunner` / `buildAsyncRunner` call `tryPlugin` / `tryAsyncPlugin`
  instead of `plugin(cmd, next)`. The sync one is a try/catch in its own
  function (tryCatchHandler's V8 shape); the async one reuses it for the
  body's throw and adds `.then(undefined, ...)` on THIS level's promise for a
  rejection, so the plugin above sees a resolved errResult. Both call
  `pluginThrew`.
- The trap found before coding: `onMissing: 'throw'` throws (sync) or rejects
  (async, via asyncMissing) out of `execute()` BELOW the chain, by contract.
  A plain boundary wrapper would relabel it VC_PLUGIN_THREW at the first
  plugin it crosses. `pluginThrew` rethrows a BusError VC_CORE_NO_HANDLER
  unchanged; pinned both buses (these two cases pass before and after: they
  guard the contract). Edge, stated: a plugin that itself rethrows a
  NO_HANDLER result's error passes through as a throw, as it did before.
- D2: `VC_PLUGIN_THREW` in BusErrorCode and ERROR_CODE_REGISTRY (severity
  error, emitter plugin, retryable false, category internal); not in
  RETRYABLE_CODES (schema.test's sync assertion holds). `cause` = the
  original, verbatim for a non-Error throw. `context` = { plugin: fn.name
  ('' for an anonymous arrow), index: position, 0 = outermost }.
- D3: DEV-only console.error naming the plugin, its index, the action and
  the fix; folded out of the IIFEs (checked in the minified core: the string
  is absent, the prod message is `Plugin threw on "<action>".`).
- syncRequest's `try { runner } catch` was dead after D1 (execute is the
  responder, never onMissing) and is removed; its test now asserts the code.
- Fixture tests/plugin-throw-fixture.test.ts (15 cases at the fails-before,
  17 now): sync + async, throw + rejected promise, outer plugin's next() and
  cleanup, afterHook / on('*') identity, query, request, non-Error cause, DEV
  log, production (no log, same result), onMissing pass-through x2, isLoading
  + lastError on the with-vapor dist, the TestBus (shares buildRunner), and
  (with /3) a pass-through over a rejecting plugin. Fails before
  (S/before.txt):
      Tests  13 failed | 2 passed (15)
- Tests moved by the change, each to a real path (no v8 ignore):
  plugins-core-coverage debounce catch and plugins-io sync()'s `.catch` now
  reach their catch via onMissing:'throw'; outbox's runFlush catch gets a new
  onMissing:'throw' replay test, and its non-Error arm is tested through a
  foreign AsyncCommandBus (flush(bus) takes any implementation; a real bus
  can no longer reject with a non-Error); command-loading-fixture's KNOWN
  LIMIT flipped to RESOLVED.
- NEW FINDING, pinned not fixed: `onMissing: 'throw'` with isLoading tracking
  starts (before-hook) and never settles, so the key stays true - probed
  (PROBEVAL=true), now a KNOWN LIMIT case in command-loading-fixture and
  stated in chamber.ts. Pre-existing, by contract (the caller chose throw);
  owner's call.
- Size trim, one measurement (finish-first): the prod message dropped the
  plugin name (kept in context and the DEV log), no 'anonymous' fallback in
  prod, the async boundary reuses tryPlugin: -10 / -17 / -7 B br.
- Gates (main tree, S/gates/1-item1b, 1-item1c): typecheck, build, test:run
  2196 passed | 1 skipped, test:vapor 24/24, coverage 100 x4
  (4983/3105/1051/4167), docs + size:doc regenerated; lint: only the five
  local-only bench markers. RED: size:check.
- Clean worktree on d807255 with a real `npm ci --ignore-scripts`
  (S/gates/1-clean-ci): typecheck, build, test:run 2196 passed, test:vapor
  24/24, coverage 100 x4, lint green ("all markers current"), docs +

  `Cannot find package 'vue/dist/vue.runtime-with-vapor.esm-browser.js'` from
